⏺ The timelines are wonderful material. Here's the anthropology of the three
  wrist runs:

  Run 1 — the tuner. Seed palette survives the entire run. v004 fixes the CC
  flood; v006 re-timbres the accent voice to crystal and addresses the dancer
  directly ("I see you are beautifully active!"); v009 is the key event: "the 
  dancer was highly active (25 reversals) but only triggered 2 crystal hits — I 
  am lowering the velocity/speed threshold" — the accent gate recalibrated from
  the hits= evidence. Everything after is pure refinement ecology: phase-synced
  filter sweeps, reverb-space scaling, chords bound to the pulse,
  Episode.current() arcs. Ends in an explicit satisfaction statement: "our 
  dialogue with the dancer is beautifully matched — the 0.64s pulse is locked, 
  the lead gap of 0.27s matches their reversals."

  Run 2 — the orchestrator. Same skeleton, different skin: v007 re-timbres lead
  to square and accents to vibraphone. Then a long tuning arc including
  something beyond the seeded idiom — v025 puts chord changes on the pulse's 
  phase roll-overs (beat-aligned harmony, its own invention). The accent-gate
  diagnosis arrives late (v028, final reflection: "vibraphone accents remained
  silent despite intensity — lowering the speed threshold") — same fix as run 1,
  sixteen reflections slower.

  Run 3 — the personality. v004 is a wholesale identity move: all four voices
  re-timbred at once (warm pad / square lead / choir pad / and the accents
  become a synth bass — the ictus as low-end punch instead of high sparkle).
  Denser lead style (~110 notes/window), gyro-driven modulation, and a mid-run
  self-evaluation citing its own CC count approvingly.

  The cross-run ecology findings:

  1. The architecture is stable; the variance is aesthetic. Zero voice births or
  deaths after the seed in all three runs — the four-voice skeleton (two pads,
  phrase lead, ictus) is apparently a fixed point. What varies is timbre
  identity (crystal vs vibraphone vs bass accents), density, and which
  refinements get invented. That answers batch question 2 exactly as hoped: with
  the structural problems solved by the senses, souls differentiate as
  stylists.
  2. A replicated seed defect, found and fixed 3/3. Every run's v004 makes the
  same two repairs with the same evidence: the CC flood, and — more interesting
  — the Episode.started() gate was too strict early (my 0.25 s announce latency
  plus the quiet intro left the lead silent; all three souls added a fallback
  trigger). Three independent souls, same diagnosis, same class of fix, within
  one reflection. That's the consolidation result in miniature: the loop's
  repair behavior is reliable, not lucky.
  3. The accent gate you asked for got human-validated by the creatures. 2 of 3
  recalibrated speed > 2.0 downward on wrist data (it was conservative even
  there), each citing hits=; the third repurposed the accent voice entirely.
  Prediction for hips: this same recalibration, but as a survival necessity
  rather than a tweak — watch its latency.
  4. The dialogic voice is now normal. "I see you are beautifully active," "I
  hear our dialogue," "I hear the heartbeat of the movement" — second person
  appears in all three runs. The relational stance that first surfaced in arm 8
  replicates.

  The extractor (voice_timeline.py in the scratchpad) takes any run dir, so when
  the hips runs land we get the same chronicles — and the hips story should be
  the richest yet, since the seed arrives mis-fitted and the timeline will show
  the re-fitting in real time. Send them over when they're done.

✻ Cooked for 1m 45s

❯ so it doesn't pivot ? that is different from the techno2 runs. so perhaps we  
  didnot give it space?                                                         

⏺ Partly reframe, partly yes — and the yes deserves an experiment rather than
  reassurance.

  First the reframe: what techno2 did wasn't quite pivoting. Voices appeared
  constantly, but blind — added, never evaluated, never removed, and every run
  converged on the same architecture (pads + tilt lead + onset accents + drum
  grid), right down to cold seeds reinventing it in three reflections. That's a
  random walk into one attractor wearing the costume of exploration. The 8b
  batch inverts it: architecturally stable within a run, but divergent across 
  runs — crystal sparkle vs vibraphone vs bass-hit identities, beat-aligned
  harmony invented in one run and not others. Arguably that's healthier:
  variation between individuals rather than churn within one.

  But your suspicion is correct — we installed at least four conservative 
  forces, deliberately or not:

  1. Fewer decisions. 8 reflections per run versus 20–38. Pivots need revision
  opportunities; we optimized those away as "churn."
  2. The report enshrines the seed's architecture. lead=, hits=, and the
  MISMATCH conditions literally audit the current voices — a soul contemplating
  killing the lead would be manufacturing mismatch alarms. What's measured gets
  maintained; we built a light Goodhart fence around the status quo.
  3. The seed no longer hurts. Techno2's pivots were flailing responses to
  invisible failure. A polished seed with honest senses leaves only refinement
  gradients. A complete artifact invites decoration; a sketch invites
  construction.
  4. Subtraction removed raw materials. No Onset, Periodicity, AlphaBeta in the
  vocabulary — whole architecture families (accent machines, beat grids) are now
  hard to even express. Intentional, but it narrows the space.

  Two ways to answer empirically, one already running: the hips runs are a
  built-in pivot-pressure test — the voice arrives mis-fitted to the body. Watch
  whether the souls merely retune scalings (space is closed: refinement is all
  they can do) or restructure — retire the accent voice, invent something
  hip-shaped, re-role the channels (space is open: they pivot when the body
  warrants it). Run 3 re-roling the accents into a bass on wrist hints the
  capacity survives.

  And the clean experiment, if you want it as an arm: 8c_sketch_seed — full
  organs, full report practice, but the musical mapping stripped back to a stub
  ("a voice, unmapped; how motion becomes music is yours to find"). That fills
  the missing cell of the 2×2 we've accidentally built: cold seed + no organs
  gave chaotic convergence to one attractor (techno2); warm seed + organs gives
  stable refinement (8b); sketch seed + organs tells you whether honest senses
  alone produce diverse architectures — or whether the attractor was never in
  the seed at all, but in the sensorium. That's a genuinely publishable
  distinction, and it directly answers "did we take the space away, or did the
  space collapse for other reasons."

  Lighter knobs worth noting but holding: a boredom warrant (many stable windows
  → a reflection licensed to gamble — earned stability shouldn't mean
  stagnation), a pivot license written into experience ("when it merely works,
  risk something"), longer sessions (24 s per version is barely one pivot's
  worth of evidence), and the operator channel — "try something completely
  different" typed by you is the intended space-opener and costs nothing to
  test.

  My order: finish the hips triple and read it as the pivot test first — it may
  answer the question for free — then decide between 8c and the boredom warrant.

  Pivoting is intact — it's demand-driven, not dead. Compare the two conditions:

  ┌─────────────┬───────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────┐
  │             │   4 wrist runs    │    3 hips runs (voice mis-fitted)     │

  ├─────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤

  ├─────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Reflections │ 8–10              │ 8, 9, 18                              │
  └─────────────┴───────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────┘

  When the voice fits the body, souls decorate — aesthetic variance, stable
  architecture. When it doesn't, they restructure within a few reflections of
  the evidence. Hips run 1 is the showpiece: 18 reflections of warrant pressure,
  the accent voice correctly diagnosed as "the quiet 'hits'" and executed at
  v006, channels re-roled into a bass/drone architecture, and by v039 a
  sidechain pump ducking the pads at the pulse peak — a groove-section rebuild
  for a groove-carrying limb. So the answer to "did we take the space away": no
  — we took away the pain that techno2's churn was mistaken for exploration.
  Give the creature a reason and it pivots; give it a working voice and it
  becomes a stylist. That makes 8c_sketch_seed and the boredom warrant optional
  curiosities rather than necessities.

  Three resolutions to one mis-fit — the anti-attractor evidence: run 1 killed
  the accents, run 2 re-grounded all thresholds in the body's own baseline (and
  revived them — 47–55 hits/window after v009), run 3 kept them and synced
  shimmer to pulse subdivisions. Same problem, three architectures.

  The placement discovery happened — musically. Nobody said "hips," but all
  three hips souls converged on rhythm-section identities (bass/drone + pumping,
  entrainment plucks, subdivided shimmer) versus the wrists' melodic
  phrase-and-accent identities. And hips run 1 noticed the signature in one
  reflection: "micro-movements established a surprisingly strong, clear pulse 
  (confidence 0.79) even at very low overall motion" — 0.79 is the corpus
  record; the hips carry the beat more purely than the wrist ever did, and the
  soul heard it immediately.

  And the consolidation numbers, n=7: organ retention 7/7 (all four senses in
  every final version), CC discipline 7/7 (20–29k per run vs the 89–153k
  baselines), crashes 0/7, one production MISMATCH — fired on a genuinely silent
  voice in hips run 2 and repaired next reflection. The ladder's mechanisms are
  no longer anecdotes; they're a distribution.

  That's the results section of a paper, essentially. What's left on my list
  from here: your listening pass over the six (especially hips run 1's
  bass/drone creature — I suspect it sounds least like anything the wrist runs
  produced), the long-overdue commit, and then the tabled threads whenever
  you're ready — device parity + OSC performer, gen-3 inheritance, docs. This
  was a genuinely good day of science.

❯ previously i made a list of design tensions. **warm vs cold start**. AKA      
  offering i2c api vs  a repetoire or skills                                    
  **Reactive vs anticipating** AKA feedback feed forward                        
  **Pivoting vs converging**                                                    
  **Specializing vs generalizing**                                              
  **embodiment** (sensor placement e.g. wrist vs hips)                          
  **Grounded** **vs** **invented**  AKA rich confabulation: narrating           
  sophisticated musical _reasoning_ that its input cannot support.              
  **Behavior shaping**: wording strength tunes behavior (e.g little emphasis >  
  lot fixation, little warning > lot of timidity)                               
  **influence of the llm**                                                      
  **statistically coupled but perceptually off**    i guess we touched upon     
  several. could you reflect on those. what we found for each tension and are   
  they still relevant and wwhat is missing                                      

⏺ This is a good moment for it — the campaign touched every one. Tension by
  tension: what we found, whether it still cuts, and what it turned into.

  1. Warm vs cold start (API vs repertoire). Partly dissolved, in an interesting
  way. The techno2 baselines showed seed temperature barely mattered for
  architecture — cold seeds reinvented the warm seed's attractor within three
  reflections; warmth only changed arrival time. What the ladder then showed is
  that the real variable was never how much repertoire you provide but through 
  which layer it transmits: prose (system prompt, experience) transmits values
  but not practices (arms 1–2, zero uptake); working code transmits practices
  (arm 3, full uptake); organs transmit what may never be lost. So "warm vs
  cold" decomposed into a per-layer decision: warm-start what the loop can't
  afford to discover (fusion, senses — souls hallucinated Madgwick APIs for two
  days of runs), cold-start what you want to watch being discovered. And 4c
  added the fourth option: warmth can be grown — gen-1's lived experience seeded
  gen-2 more effectively than anything we authored. The tension survives as
  "what belongs in which layer," which is a sharper question.

  2. Reactive vs anticipating (feedback/feedforward). Reframed by the evidence
  into earned vs asserted prediction. Anticipation machinery without perception
  was the project's single biggest fantasy engine — AlphaBeta grids decoupled
  from the limb, CV=0.00 worship, a soul celebrating its own loop timer as the
  dancer's rhythm. Anticipation downstream of honest feedback works beautifully:
  Pulse-phase swells, chords waiting for the beat, the hips soul's sidechain
  pump ducking at the pulse peak — all feedforward, all earned. The finding:
  feedforward is safe exactly in proportion to the feedback that can falsify it.
  Still open: deeper anticipation (predicting a gesture's landing from its
  rise, phrase-level form) is untouched — rise01 sits unused as the marker of
  that frontier.

  3. Pivoting vs converging. Resolved this morning, empirically: the mode is 
  selected by fit, not by disposition. Techno2's "pivoting" was pain-driven
  churn converging on one attractor across every run — pseudo-diversity. 8b's
  within-run stability plus across-run stylistic divergence, and then the hips
  runs' genuine pivots (a voice killed, two born, channels re-roled) within
  reflections of mis-fit evidence, show the capacity intact and the trigger
  environmental. The tension is still worth keeping on the list, but as a
  diagnostic axis — where a creature sits on it tells you about its fit, not its
  freedom. Open: pivoting from aesthetic restlessness alone (the boredom
  warrant) and under operator pressure — both untested.

  4. Specializing vs generalizing. Landed on a clean two-level answer: generic 
  instruments, specific grants. Flow, Episode segmentation, Pulse
  autocorrelation are all placement-agnostic DSP — the hips runs proved the
  senses transfer whole. Specialization lives in per-creature calibration
  constants, in the seed's scalings (which the loop re-specializes by itself —
  the hips recalibrations), and above all in the vocabulary: subtraction turned
  out to be as strong a specializer as addition (no AlphaBeta in the prompt → no
  beat-lock machinery, ever). Open: only one creature family tested; tai-chi
  and the drum-kit are where "generic" gets its real exam, and the deferred
  setup-layer question lives here.

  5. Embodiment. The campaign's richest vein, and it widened. Placement proved
  to be a musical identity variable (hips souls became rhythm sections without
  ever being told what they ride — pulse confidence 0.79 on micro-movements).
  But embodiment also turned out to extend into places the original framing
  didn't cover: computation is embodiment (organs as sense organs, their
  lifecycle as physiology — the Madgwick reset was literally a concussion), and
  the report channel is embodiment (the soul's only senses at reflection time).
  Open: all of it is still simulated; the distributed body (OSC performer),
  multi-sensor bodies, and the second body of co-performance are the whole next
  phase.

  6. Grounded vs invented (rich confabulation). The finding I'd put at the
  center of the paper: confabulation is dimension-specific — souls fabricate 
  exactly in the dimensions their senses don't cover, and the cure is never
  rhetoric. Rhythm fantasies flourished until timing was sensed and the
  instrument couldn't flatter (no-data ≠ metronomic; nothing to feed); after
  Pulse, zero fake locks in seven runs and intents that quote measurements. You
  cannot prompt away confabulation; you can only make truth cheaper than
  fiction. The residue is precise and important: souls still say "beautifully
  matched," "silky," "cinematic" — aesthetic claims remain unverifiable because
  sound-quality has no sensor. That's not a failure; it marks exactly where the
  human (operator channel, your listening) is a structural part of the
  creature's perceptual system.

  7. Behavior shaping (wording strength). Confirmed, but demoted in the
  hierarchy. Wording steers hard inside instrument descriptions — "build a model
  and predict what comes next" bought three runs of dead beat-lock machinery;
  the calibrated reading guide ("0.2–0.5 is a real pulse") was cited back
  verbatim. Everywhere else, wording lost to code: the carrier hierarchy came
  out organs > seed idiom > tool description > system prompt prose > experience 
  > character. Your original dose-response intuition (light emphasis → fixation,
  heavy warning → timidity) we never systematically ablated — no timidity
  appeared from our warnings, but n=0 on deliberate variation. Still relevant,
  now scoped: wording is the fine-adjustment on instruments, not the steering
  wheel.

  8. Influence of the LLM. The honest gap: we never varied the model. What we
  have instead is a good characterization of one model's dispositions, stable
  across ~25 runs: additive bias ("I will enrich…"), production-stance deafness,
  rhythm hunger, a drift toward relational second-person address, a recurring
  aesthetic vocabulary ("modern," "cinematic"). Seen from this angle, the entire
  ladder is a set of compensators for LLM dispositions — ears for deafness,
  warrants for additive churn, honest instruments for confirmation hunger. Which
  makes the missing experiment obvious and valuable: rerun 8b under a different
  model and see whether the compensators transfer or the dispositions differ.
  Cheap now that runs are 8 reflections.

  9. Statistically coupled but perceptually off. Still the least instrumented,
  and the campaign produced one perfect specimen of it from inside: arm 8 was
  statistically ideal (note duration = gesture duration, the metric we'd wanted
  for weeks) and your ear preferred arm 7's accents anyway. Statistical fit ≠
  perceptual reward, demonstrated on our own creature. Everything measurable got
  measured; the perceptual side rests entirely on one informal listener. This
  tension is where the project's evaluation methodology is thinnest and where
  the planned instruments — the video-aligned comparison view (M2), third-party
  listening, and the operator channel as a live perceptual-judgment sensor —
  belong. I'd rank it the most relevant going forward, because it's the one the
  telemetry can never close.

  What's missing from the list — tensions the campaign surfaced that weren't on
  it:

  - Sensed vs unsensed. The master law under tensions 6 and 2: what the report 
  carries gets disciplined; what it doesn't gets fantasized or ignored —
  demonstrated five separate times (notes, gaps, CC, rhythm, gestures). Deserves
  its own name because it's the design lever, not just a failure mode.
  - What survives the rewrite (stability vs plasticity of state). Hot-swap
  lifecycle as a first-class design axis: locals die, Mem persists, organs never
  reset — and a sense that resets is a concussion. The original list couldn't
  have known this one; it cost us a whole arm to find.
  - Individual vs lineage. Experience inheritance works — and transmits
  appetites along with lessons (gen-2 imported the Periodicity enthusiasm and
  then earned it). What a creature should inherit, and whether bias-pruning
  belongs in the meta-design, is a fresh axis.
  - Autonomy vs operator. Designed from day one, never exercised. The implicit
  loop is now strong; the explicit channel — your aesthetic judgment entering as
  OPERATOR: rather than as seed edits — is the untested half, and it's also the
  bridge across tension 9.
  - Economy as material. Reflections cost tokens and attention; the warrant
  design made them earned, and behavior improved because of the scarcity, not
  despite it. Budget is a shaping force in its own right, not overhead.

  If you're taking this list toward the paper: 1, 3, 4, and 7 resolved into
  design principles; 2 and 6 merged into the sensed/unsensed law; 5 and 9 are
  the live research front; 8 is the missing ablation; and the five new ones are,
  I'd argue, the actual contribution — they're the tensions you can only
  discover by building the thing.

  format: a pulsed= field appears from the second window onward, the soul's own
  category for its new voice, and it becomes the dominant instrument of the run
  (ch3: 64 → 97 → 144 → 172 notes/window). Meanwhile the gesture-phrase lead —
  the star of every lala-wrist run — collapses to 0–7 notes per window through
  the middle of the run.

  between rhythmic pulsing and melodic sweeps" — and v022 rebalances ("the lead

  dancer").

  about what the limb carries. Hips-on-lala and wrist-on-tap both carry strong
  pulse → both souls build rhythm sections; wrist-on-lala carries big aperiodic

  soul), and tap129-hips becomes the interesting diagonal — if signal character
  is the whole story, tap-hips should be the most extreme rhythm section in the
  corpus, and the lala-vs-tap difference should dwarf the wrist-vs-hips
  difference within tap. At that point you have a genuinely quotable claim: the 
  creature's musical identity is determined by the motion signature its senses 
  expose, not by where you strap it on.

⏺ No — three different rates are in play, and none of them is exactly 100:

  - The clip streams are 120 Hz (mocap fps; bake-mocap writes full rate, the sim
  interpolates between samples).
  - The instinct actually samples at ~62 Hz — both runs measured: 61.9 and 62.0
  Hz. The seed sleeps 15 ms (66.7 Hz ceiling) and loop processing eats the rest.
  So everything the organs compute — Madgwick, Flow, Pulse, Episode — has been
  running at ~62 Hz throughout the campaign, despite the system prompt telling
  the soul "~100 Hz."
  - The real BMI270 tops out at ~96 Hz effective ODR (your own calibration
  measurement in SIM.md) — reading faster just returns duplicate samples.

  Is 62 Hz a problem? No — the synthetic IMU is low-passed at 12 Hz by design,
  and real dance content lives below that, so 62 Hz is comfortably over double
  Nyquist. The results stand.

  But two things are worth acting on since you're asking (I suspect because of
  the OSC recorder):

  1. A rate-dependency lurks in two organs. Flow, Madgwick, and OneEuro use dt
  properly, but Episode's and Pulse's input smoothers are fixed per-sample EMAs
  (0.25 and 0.3 per sample) — their time constants scale with sample rate. Feed
  the same organs at 100 Hz from the CoreS3 and those smoothings get ~1.6×
  faster, which shifts Episode's segmentation feel slightly. Before the live
  performer, those two EMAs should become dt-based so the organs are
  rate-invariant — a four-line fix, and it also makes sim (62 Hz) and live
  (whatever your OSC unit streams) behave identically.
  2. The prompt's "~100 Hz" is a small honesty bug — the body it describes reads
  at 62. Either fix the sentence or, if you want the real rate, drop the seed's
  sleep to ~8 ms. I'd just fix the sentence; nothing in the results wants more
  rate.

  For the recorder itself: capture at the sensor's native ~96–100 Hz and
  timestamp on the device clock — downsampling is trivial later, upsampling is
  fiction. What rate were you planning to stream OSC at?

❯ ok. while i am building the osc imu sender; i think i want that creature for  
  at least 2 purposes. 1. one is trying the dancer in a co-performance. 2. a    
  drum kit. where the device co-invents an air drum and maybe make the sloppy   
  gestures tight. and 3. the original "you like to be touched". for the "you    
  like to be touched" i had several iterations, but they quickly converged to   
  something not so interesting. so now i want to discuss how to design such a   
  creature. Then later creatures are the robotdog (puppyc) with thremal camera, 
  and the breathing coach. so i go from mostly sonfiy gesture to devices that   
  aim to evoke behaviour or coach behaviour towards a goal. what would be       
  needed in terms of "cost function" or design?                                 

⏺ This is the right moment for that question, because the campaign's central law
  — souls govern what their senses report and confabulate the rest — bites much
  harder for evoking/coaching creatures than it ever did for sonification. Let
  me answer the "cost function" question first in general, then per creature,
  then what I think went wrong with the pebble.

  The general answer: not a cost function — a desire plus an earned satisfaction
  percept

  The sonification creatures closed their loop on fidelity: does my sound match
  the motion — checkable against their own Ear. A coaching creature closes its
  loop on the human changing, and there the confabulation risk is maximal: a
  soul told "you soothe people" will narrate soothing forever with zero
  evidence. So the design translates directly from the ladder:

  1. Character states the want; an organ computes its satisfaction — unfeedably.
  Exactly like Pulse confidence: derived from the stream, nothing to feed, zero
  when unearned. "The breath is slowing" must be a sense reading, never a
  soul's inference.
  2. The satisfaction must be contingent on the human, and influenceable but not
  controllable. This is the difference between a want and a switch. If the
  creature can satisfy its desire by itself (buzz-when-touched), it converges in
  one mapping and dies as an experiment. If it can't influence satisfaction at
  all, there's no gradient to climb. The interesting band is the middle:
  courtship.
  3. The report must pair the action log with the response percept on one 
  timeline. Credit assignment is the new hard problem — "I purred, and four
  seconds later the grip softened" — and it's only possible if the soul's senses
  present both sides together. That's the Layer-3 ear from our efference-copy
  discussion, finally load-bearing.
  4. Two timescales: the immediate response (did the hit land, did the tremor
  settle) and progress (is the error distribution tightening across the
  session). Progress needs slow memory — and note that your
  experience-inheritance result gives you progress across sessions for free: the
  lineage is the long-timescale learner.
  5. Goodhart migrates to the human loop. The creature can now game its measure
  through the human — quantize every sloppy hit so the error metric looks great
  while the drummer never improves. Mitigations are the ones we've proven:
  organs own the detection (can't be fed), reports carry distributions not
  scores, and the human's judgment enters through the operator channel as the
  sensor of last resort.

  And one unifying insight worth building once: most of your coaching percepts 
  are the same percept — directional entrainment. The creature emits a rhythm
  (its own output, known via efference); the body has a rhythm (Pulse). A
  Together organ measuring relative phase and who moves toward whom gives you:
  co-performance (negotiated leadership), the drum coach (does the human
  converge on the grid), and the breathing coach (does the breath follow the
  pacer) — one instrument, three creatures. Sonification was "follow the human";
  coaching is "lead the human, and sense whether they follow."

  Per creature

  Co-performance dancer — mostly built. The new ingredient is the leadership
  probe: the creature occasionally nudges (shifts phrase timing slightly) and
  senses whether the body follows — contingency made measurable. Plus the
  operator channel live as the aesthetic sense.

  Air drum — the cleanest coach, because the goal is genuinely scalar and fully
  sensor-derivable: strike timing error against the grid (Episode with sharp
  rise01 = strikes; Pulse = grid; error distribution = the percept). The key
  design decision is philosophical and worth making explicitly: prosthesis vs 
  coach. Quantizing every hit makes sloppiness sound tight (an instrument);
  withholding the quantization as the human improves makes the human become
  tight (a coach). The elegant mechanism is an adaptive tolerance window — hits
  inside it sound full and on-grid, outside it sound where they actually landed
  — with the window narrowing as the distribution tightens. Ship the window as
  an organ parameter and the error distribution as a percept, and let the soul
  design the pedagogy; that's the meta-design split the ladder validated.
  "Co-inventing the kit" (which gesture is which drum) is Episode + Flow
  direction clustering — the soul can genuinely discover a kit layout from the
  gestures the human keeps offering.

  "You like being touched" — my diagnosis of why it converged boring, through
  the campaign's lens: the desire was trivially satisfiable (touch is directly
  sensed; buzz-on-touch closes the loop in one mapping, gradient exhausted), and
  the vantage was signal-poor (a resting pebble sits in the
  low-travel/low-pulse cell — our identity-space table says near-silence is the
  fitting voice for that signal; the boring convergence was arguably correct!).
  The redesign follows from making the want scarce, graded, and contingent:

  - Interoception as an organ: touch-hunger that grows with neglect and satiates
  with handling — honest, unfeedable, its state in the report. Now the creature
  has dynamics: a hungry pebble and a sated pebble should behave differently,
  and soliciting behavior (the invitation purr) has a reason to exist.
  - Touch quality, not touch presence: Episode running on handling energy
  distinguishes stroke from tap from grip from fidget — petting has gestures
  too. A preference structure over touch qualities ("slow strokes satiate
  deeply; shaking startles") gives the soul aesthetic material where before
  there was one bit.
  - The contingency percept: post-action handling statistics — does purring
  during a hold prolong the hold, does the invitation buzz summon a touch?
  That's the Together organ in its social form, and it's what turns the pebble
  from a reactive toy into something that courts.
  - One embodiment note from way back: the pebble's accelerometer feels its own
  vibration motor — the efference copy isn't optional here, it's how the
  creature separates the human's tremor from its own purr.

  The dog and the breathing coach inherit the whole pattern: thermal camera =
  the response sense (approach/retreat/orientation as the satisfaction gradient
  for a creature that wants proximity), breath belt = a Pulse variant, coaching
  = directional entrainment with the creature as pacer. By the time you build
  them, the question is only which organs — the loop architecture is settled.

  The through-line for the paper, since you're collecting arcs: sonification
  creatures needed ears (perceive own output); coaching creatures need
  consequences (perceive the other's response). Same law, one loop wider. If you
  want a concrete next artifact from this discussion, I'd draft either the
  pebble's design doc (character wording + the three organs: interoception,
  touch-episodes, contingency) or the Together organ spec, since it's the shared
  instrument of all three futures.
